```
=== #536 commit (workspace packages fix) ===
commit ac71fef0068a7da1393994a4c73e0b84956d8b13
Author: Michael Yankelev <12774278+FSM1@users.noreply.github.com>
Date:   Sun Jun 21 02:28:37 2026 +0200

    fix(e2e): make desktop e2e helper dirs workspace packages (#536)
    
    Phase 54 migrated the e2e helper scripts to TypeScript, adding scripts under
    tests/desktop-e2e/scripts and tests/e2e-helpers that import @cipherbox/* packages.
    Neither directory had a package.json, so although pnpm-workspace.yaml globs tests/*,
    they were not real workspace packages and had no node_modules with the @cipherbox
    deps. tsconfig.scripts.json maps those paths for typecheck, but tsx does not use that
    config at runtime, so 'pnpm exec tsx <script>' failed with
    ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND: Cannot find package '@cipherbox/sdk-core'. Desktop E2E runs on
    main push (not PRs), so this only surfaced after the phase 52/54 merge landed.
    
    Add a package.json to each dir, matching the existing @cipherbox/web-e2e and
    @cipherbox/sdk-e2e suites, declaring the @cipherbox/* deps they import so pnpm
    symlinks them into node_modules and tsx resolves them at runtime:
      - @cipherbox/desktop-e2e: sdk-core, crypto
      - @cipherbox/e2e-helpers:  api-client, sdk-core
    
    This unblocks every failing scenario (round-trip, cross-client-sync, move-content),
    whose verify/edit/rename helpers transitively load tests/e2e-helpers/auth.ts.
    
    
    Entire-Checkpoint: 022ad0f648b4
    
    Co-authored-by: Claude Opus 4.8 <noreply@anthropic.com>
```

> AGENT

**Root cause found and confirmed.** `@cipherbox/core`'s CJS build (`packages/core/dist/index.js` lines 777, 814) does `require("ipns")`, but `ipns@10` is ESM-only — its `exports["."]` map has only `types`+`import` conditions, no `require`/`default`. So a CJS `require("ipns")` throws `ERR_PACKAGE_PATH_NOT_EXPORTED: No "exports" main defined`.

Why it's CJS: the helpers `verify-filepointer.ts`/`edit-filepointer.ts`/`rename-folder.ts` live in `packages/sdk-core/scripts/`, and `packages/sdk-core` has **no `"type": "module"`**. So tsx runs them as CommonJS → `import` becomes `require` → loads core's CJS dist → `require("ipns")` → crash. The pre-#532 `.mjs` helpers ran as ESM (loading core's `.mjs`, which does `import "ipns"` → works). `@cipherbox/crypto` loads fine because its CJS dist doesn't `require("ipns")`.

`tests/e2e-helpers` and `tests/desktop-e2e` both already have `"type": "module"` — `packages/sdk-core/scripts/` is the gap. Let me check the dir contents and verify the fix.

> TOOL

Progress — the ipns error is gone, but a bare `{"type":"module"}` shadows `packages/sdk-core`'s package.json, breaking ESM **self-referencing** of `@cipherbox/sdk-core` (the script imports its own parent package). The scope package.json loses its `name`+`exports`. 

Cleaner fix: rename the helpers to `.mts` (ESM by extension) so the package scope stays `packages/sdk-core/package.json` and self-reference still works. Let me test that.

> AGENT

Fixed and committed on `fix/desktop-e2e-ipns-cjs-esm-resolution` (commit `dbb293b4f`). lint-staged's eslint/prettier passed.

## Root cause

The desktop E2E was failing on **every** helper invocation (round-trip, cross-client-sync, move-content) with:

```
ERR_PACKAGE_PATH_NOT_EXPORTED: No "exports" main defined in
  packages/core/node_modules/ipns/package.json
```

The chain:
- The verify/edit/rename helpers live in `packages/sdk-core/scripts/`, and `packages/sdk-core` has **no `"type": "module"`**.
- #532 migrated them `.mjs → .ts`. So `tsx` now runs them as **CommonJS** → their `import`s become `require()`.
- That loads `@cipherbox/core`'s **CJS** build (`dist/index.js`), which does `require("ipns")`.
- `ipns@10` is **ESM-only** — its `exports["."]` has only `types`+`import`, no `require`/`default`. CJS `require` → the error above.

The pre-#532 `.mjs` helpers worked because they ran as ESM, loading core's `.mjs` build (`import "ipns"`). So #536 fixed the *first* error (`@cipherbox/sdk-core not found`); this CJS/ESM one was the *next* layer underneath, which is why main still fails. (The branch in your link, `fix/desktop-e2e-helper-script-module-resolution`, is byte-identical to main — it doesn't address this.)

## Fix

Rename the three helpers `.ts → .mts` so `tsx` runs them as ESM regardless of the package's CJS type, while keeping the package scope intact (so the `@cipherbox/sdk-core` self-reference still resolves). Updated the `.sh`/`.ps1` runners + `test-move-content.ts`, and added `packages/sdk-core/scripts/*.mts` to `tsconfig.scripts.json` so they stay typechecked.

## Verified locally
- All 3 helpers now load past imports under the exact `pnpm exec tsx …` CI uses (reach arg parser, no `ipns` error).
- `tsc -p tsconfig.scripts.json` passes and `--listFiles` confirms the `.mts` files are in scope (no silent untyped drift).
- eslint clean on all three.

Want me to push the branch and open the PR to main?

```json
{
  "command": "cd /Users/myankelev/Code/random/cipher-box\ncat > /tmp/cb_pr_body.md <<'EOF'\n## Problem\n\nDesktop E2E fails on every helper invocation (round-trip, cross-client-sync, move-content) with:\n\n```\nERR_PACKAGE_PATH_NOT_EXPORTED: No \"exports\" main defined in\n  packages/core/node_modules/ipns/package.json\n```\n\nChain:\n\n- The verify/edit/rename helpers live in `packages/sdk-core/scripts/`, and `packages/sdk-core` has no `\"type\": \"module\"`.\n- `#532` migrated them `.mjs` -> `.ts`, so `tsx` now runs them as **CommonJS** and their `import`s become `require()`.\n- That loads `@cipherbox/core`'s **CJS** build (`dist/index.js`), which does `require(\"ipns\")`.\n- `ipns@10` is **ESM-only**: its `exports[\".\"]` map has only `types` + `import` conditions, no `require`/`default`. A CJS `require` therefore throws the error above.\n\nThe pre-`#532` `.mjs` helpers worked because they ran as ESM and loaded core's `.mjs` build (`import \"ipns\"`). `#536` fixed the first error (`@cipherbox/sdk-core` not found); this CJS/ESM layer was underneath it, which is why main still fails after that merge.\n\n## Fix\n\nRename the three helpers `.ts` -> `.mts` so `tsx` runs them as ESM regardless of the package's CommonJS type, while keeping the package scope intact (so the `@cipherbox/sdk-core` self-reference still resolves). Update the `.sh`/`.ps1` runners and `test-move-content.ts` to the new paths, and add `packages/sdk-core/scripts/*.mts` to `tsconfig.scripts.json` so the helpers stay typechecked.\n\n## Verification\n\n- All 3 helpers load past imports under the exact `pnpm exec tsx ...` invocation CI uses (they reach their arg parser; no `ipns` error).\n- `pnpm exec tsc -p tsconfig.scripts.json --noEmit` passes; `--listFiles` confirms the `.mts` files are in scope, so they are not silently dropped from typechecking.\n- `eslint` clean on all three.\n\n🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)\nEOF\nenv -u GITHUB_TOKEN gh pr create \\\n  --repo FSM1/cipher-box \\\n  --base main \\\n  --head fix/desktop-e2e-ipns-cjs-esm-resolution \\\n  --title \"fix(e2e): run sdk-core e2e helpers as esm to load esm-only ipns\" \\\n  --body-file /tmp/cb_pr_body.md 2>&1 | tail -5\nrm -f /tmp/cb_pr_body.md",
  "description": "Create PR to main"
}
```

```
    fix(e2e): run sdk-core e2e helpers as esm to load esm-only ipns
    
    The desktop E2E verify/edit/rename helpers live in packages/sdk-core/scripts,
    which has no "type": "module". After #532 migrated them from .mjs to .ts, tsx
    ran them as CommonJS, so their imports became require(). That loaded
    @cipherbox/core's CJS build (dist/index.js), which does require("ipns").
    
    ipns@10 is ESM-only: its exports map "." has only types+import conditions and
    no require/default. A CJS require therefore throws
    ERR_PACKAGE_PATH_NOT_EXPORTED: No "exports" main defined, killing every helper
    invocation and failing the round-trip, cross-client-sync, and move-content
    desktop E2E scenarios. The pre-#532 .mjs helpers worked because they ran as
    ESM and loaded core's .mjs build, which does import "ipns".
    
    Rename the three helpers to .mts so tsx runs them as ESM regardless of the
    package's CommonJS type, keeping the package scope (and @cipherbox/sdk-core
    self-reference) intact. Update the .sh/.ps1 runners and test-move-content.ts to
    the new paths, and add packages/sdk-core/scripts/*.mts to tsconfig.scripts.json
    so the helpers stay typechecked.
```

```
60	
61	// Nudge the mount so the FUSE/WinFsp layer re-resolves metadata (mirrors the
62	// `ls`/`stat` the shell harness used between polls). Best-effort.
63	function nudge(...paths: string[]): void {
64	  for (const p of paths) {
65	    try {
66	      statSync(p);
67	    } catch {
68	      /* not visible yet */
69	    }
70	    try {
71	      readdirSync(p);
72	    } catch {
73	      /* not a dir / not visible yet */
74	    }
75	  }
76	}
77	
78	// Read a file back via a FRESH SDK client and assert its decrypted content.
79	// Returns true only when verify-filepointer.ts exits 0 (content matched under
80	// the resolved folderKey).
81	function verify({
82	  fileName,
83	  folderName,
84	  content,
85	}: {
86	  fileName: string;
87	  folderName?: string;
88	  content: string;
89	}): boolean {
90	  const cliArgs = [
91	    VERIFY_SCRIPT,
92	    '--api-url',
93	    apiUrl,
94	    '--email',
95	    TEST_EMAIL,
96	    '--file-name',
97	    fileName,
98	    '--expected-content',
99	    content,
100	  ];
101	  if (folderName) {
102	    cliArgs.push('--folder-name', folderName);
103	  }
104	  // process.execPath (node) cannot run a .ts file directly, so spawn the tsx
105	  // shim from node_modules/.bin as the interpreter for verify-filepointer.ts.
106	  const result = spawnSync('node', [join(REPO_ROOT, 'node_modules/.bin/tsx'), ...cliArgs], {
107	    env: { ...process.env, TEST_SECRET: secret },
108	    encoding: 'utf8',
109	    // Bound the child: a stalled verify must not hang the whole suite — pollVerify
110	    // cannot recover from a blocking spawnSync. A timeout returns a non-zero status
111	    // so the poll loop treats it as a failed attempt and retries.
112	    timeout: 60_000,
113	    maxBuffer: 1024 * 1024,
114	  });
115	  if (result.error) {
116	    console.error(`verify-filepointer failed to execute: ${result.error.message}`);
117	    return false;
118	  }
119	  return result.status === 0;
120	}
121	
122	async function pollVerify(
123	  label: string,
124	  opts: { fileName: string; folderName?: string; content: string },
125	  nudgePaths: string[]
126	): Promise<boolean> {
127	  const ATTEMPTS = 24;
128	  const DELAY_MS = 5000;
129	  for (let attempt = 1; attempt <= ATTEMPTS; attempt += 1) {
130	    nudge(...nudgePaths);
131	    await sleep(DELAY_MS);
132	    if (verify(opts)) {
133	      return true;
134	    }
135	  }
136	  console.error(`FAIL: ${label} did not verify after ${ATTEMPTS} attempts`);
137	  return false;
138	}
139	
140	async function main(): Promise<void> {
141	  // Math.random/Date are fine here (this is a plain Node script, not a workflow).
142	  const tag = `${process.pid}-${Date.now()}`;
143	  const moveFile = `move-reencrypt-${tag}.txt`;
144	  const moveSubdir = `move-dest-${tag}`;
145	  const moveContent = 'content must survive re-encryption on move';
146	
147	  console.log('=== Move-content E2E (cross-platform) ===');
148	  console.log(`Mount point: ${mount}`);
149	  console.log(`API URL:     ${apiUrl}`);
150	  console.log(`File:        ${moveFile} -> ${moveSubdir}/${moveFile}`);
151	
152	  let failed = false;
153	  try {
154	    writeFileSync(join(mount, moveFile), moveContent);
155	    mkdirSync(join(mount, moveSubdir), { recursive: true });
156	    await sleep(2000);
157	    nudge(mount);
158	
159	    // 1. Confirm initial encryption is good: file readable via the SDK at root.
160	    if (
161	      !(await pollVerify('pre-move root verify', { fileName: moveFile, content: moveContent }, [
162	        mount,
163	      ]))
164	    ) {
165	      console.error('FAIL: file not decryptable via SDK before move');
166	      failed = true;
167	    } else {
168	      // 2. Move THROUGH THE MOUNT (fs.renameSync == the OS rename the FUSE/WinFsp
169	      //    rename handler services; it must re-encrypt FileMetadata to the dest key).
170	      renameSync(join(mount, moveFile), join(mount, moveSubdir, moveFile));
171	      await sleep(3000);
172	      nudge(mount, join(mount, moveSubdir));
173	
174	      // 3. Read back via a FRESH SDK client under the DESTINATION folderKey.
175	      if (
176	        await pollVerify(
177	          'post-move subfolder verify',
178	          { fileName: moveFile, folderName: moveSubdir, content: moveContent },
179	          [join(mount, moveSubdir)]
180	        )
181	      ) {
182	        console.log('PASS: moved file decrypts under destination folderKey (re-encryption OK)');
183	      } else {
184	        console.error(
185	          'FAIL: moved file not decryptable under destination key (re-encryption failed)'
186	        );
187	        failed = true;
188	      }
189	    }
190	  } finally {
191	    try {
192	      rmSync(join(mount, moveSubdir), { recursive: true, force: true });
193	    } catch {
194	      /* best-effort cleanup */
195	    }
196	    try {
197	      rmSync(join(mount, moveFile), { force: true });
198	    } catch {
199	      /* best-effort cleanup */
200	    }
201	  }
202	
203	  process.exit(failed ? 1 : 0);
204	}
205	
206	main().catch((error) => {
207	  console.error(error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error));
208	  process.exit(1);
209	});
```

> AGENT

Decisive evidence from the move window (01:08): the file **was** uploaded and published (`File uploaded: ino 16`, `Per-file IPNS publish succeeded`, `Parent metadata published after mkdir`), and root IPNS churned `seq 29 → 33`. So this isn't "FUSE didn't process the write" — it's that the **final root folder metadata the fresh SDK resolves doesn't contain the file**. The write-to-root and the near-simultaneous mkdir both republish root parent metadata, and one clobbers the other's entry — a publish-coordination race.

That points at #533 (the PublishCoordinator/journal work), possibly *exposed* by #532's test doing `write`+`mkdir` back-to-back. Let me fan out a parallel investigation to pin ownership precisely.

> AGENT

Final E2E result is in: **Web E2E passes; all 3 desktop platforms fail** — consistently on the same move-content step (linux + macos confirmed identical; windows same conclusion).

So, holding the merge — the E2E isn't green, per your instruction. Where things stand:

- ✅ **My fix works.** The module-resolution regression is gone; round-trip and cross-client-sync (previously failing on `ipns`) now pass. Failures dropped 4 → 1.
- ❌ **move-content** fails on all 3 platforms, consistently (not flaky). It's a **separate** defect from the module-resolution fix.
- This matches the history: desktop E2E has been red since #532/#533 landed — the "green" main pushes simply **skipped** desktop (path filter); only `workflow_dispatch` forces it to run.

The forensics already point to a **publish-coordination race**: in the move scenario, the root file-write and the near-simultaneous `mkdir` both republish the root folder's IPNS metadata, and the final root version the SDK resolves is missing the file entry (log shows the file uploaded + published, root seq churning `29→33`, then re-resolution). cross-client-sync passes because it doesn't do a concurrent root write + mkdir.

I've launched a parallel investigation (`woxzh2d00`) to attribute it precisely to **#532 (test rewrite)** vs **#533 (FUSE hardening)** with a fix + scope recommendation. I'll report the verdict when it lands — then you can decide whether move-content gets fixed in this PR or a separate `fix(...)` PR.

> AGENT

Verified against source — the conclusion holds, with one correction to the synthesis:

- **#533 *did* substantially touch the publish machinery** — `lib.rs +511`, `journal_helpers.rs +152`, `read_ops.rs +84`, `write_ops.rs +7` (the synthesis overstated "#533 didn't touch it"). So #533 is entangled with the race surface even if it didn't originate it.
- **The race itself is real and product-side** — I read the mkdir `Success` arm in `write_ops.rs`: it does a blind `coordinator.record_publish(parent, new_seq)` with **no merge**; merge only happens on a 409 conflict, which never fires because both publishers serialize on the same coordinator lock (so the second gets a valid sequence and succeeds). If the mkdir's root snapshot was taken before the file was added to `root.children`, the file is silently dropped from the published root.
- **move-content test** was created in the #532 migration (from a `.mjs` predecessor); the back-to-back `write`+`mkdir` is what triggers the latent race.

## Bottom line

The move-content failure is a **genuine FUSE publish-coordination lost-update bug** (data-loss class) — **not** caused by my module-resolution fix, **not** a test-logic bug, and present regardless of which suspect PR you blame. cross-client-sync passes because it never does a concurrent root write + mkdir.

So **#537 is correct and complete** for what it targets. Desktop E2E won't go fully green until the FUSE race is fixed separately — which means your "merge when E2E is green" condition can't be met by #537 alone.

Two cheap things I'd fold into #537 regardless (still `chore(e2e)`, zero bumps): print the verifier's swallowed stdout/stderr on failure (`test-move-content.ts:119`) so the next regression self-diagnoses.

How do you want to proceed?

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,

  "old_string": "function verify({\n  fileName,\n  folderName,\n  content,\n}: {\n  fileName: string;\n  folderName?: string;\n  content: string;\n}): boolean {\n  const cliArgs = [\n    VERIFY_SCRIPT,\n    '--api-url',\n    apiUrl,\n    '--email',\n    TEST_EMAIL,\n    '--file-name',\n    fileName,\n    '--expected-content',\n    content,\n  ];\n  if (folderName) {\n    cliArgs.push('--folder-name', folderName);\n  }\n  // process.execPath (node) cannot run a .ts file directly, so spawn the tsx\n  // shim from node_modules/.bin as the interpreter for verify-filepointer.ts.\n  const result = spawnSync('node', [join(REPO_ROOT, 'node_modules/.bin/tsx'), ...cliArgs], {\n    env: { ...process.env, TEST_SECRET: secret },\n    encoding: 'utf8',\n    // Bound the child: a stalled verify must not hang the whole suite — pollVerify\n    // cannot recover from a blocking spawnSync. A timeout returns a non-zero status\n    // so the poll loop treats it as a failed attempt and retries.\n    timeout: 60_000,\n    maxBuffer: 1024 * 1024,\n  });\n  if (result.error) {\n    console.error(`verify-filepointer failed to execute: ${result.error.message}`);\n    return false;\n  }\n  return result.status === 0;\n}\n\nasync function pollVerify(\n  label: string,\n  opts: { fileName: string; folderName?: string; content: string },\n  nudgePaths: string[]\n): Promise<boolean> {\n  const ATTEMPTS = 24;\n  const DELAY_MS = 5000;\n  for (let attempt = 1; attempt <= ATTEMPTS; attempt += 1) {\n    nudge(...nudgePaths);\n    await sleep(DELAY_MS);\n    if (verify(opts)) {\n      return true;\n    }\n  }\n  console.error(`FAIL: ${label} did not verify after ${ATTEMPTS} attempts`);\n  return false;\n}",
  "new_string": "function verify({\n  fileName,\n  folderName,\n  content,\n}: {\n  fileName: string;\n  folderName?: string;\n  content: string;\n}): { ok: boolean; output: string } {\n  const cliArgs = [\n    VERIFY_SCRIPT,\n    '--api-url',\n    apiUrl,\n    '--email',\n    TEST_EMAIL,\n    '--file-name',\n    fileName,\n    '--expected-content',\n    content,\n  ];\n  if (folderName) {\n    cliArgs.push('--folder-name', folderName);\n  }\n  // process.execPath (node) cannot run a .ts file directly, so spawn the tsx\n  // shim from node_modules/.bin as the interpreter for verify-filepointer.mts.\n  const result = spawnSync('node', [join(REPO_ROOT, 'node_modules/.bin/tsx'), ...cliArgs], {\n    env: { ...process.env, TEST_SECRET: secret },\n    encoding: 'utf8',\n    // Bound the child: a stalled verify must not hang the whole suite — pollVerify\n    // cannot recover from a blocking spawnSync. A timeout returns a non-zero status\n    // so the poll loop treats it as a failed attempt and retries.\n    timeout: 60_000,\n    maxBuffer: 1024 * 1024,\n  });\n  if (result.error) {\n    return { ok: false, output: `verify-filepointer failed to execute: ${result.error.message}` };\n  }\n  // The verifier never prints secrets (it refuses --secret; TEST_SECRET is env-only),\n  // so its stdout/stderr are safe to surface for diagnostics on final failure.\n  const output = [result.stdout, result.stderr].filter(Boolean).join('\\n').trim();\n  return { ok: result.status === 0, output };\n}\n\nasync function pollVerify(\n  label: string,\n  opts: { fileName: string; folderName?: string; content: string },\n  nudgePaths: string[]\n): Promise<boolean> {\n  const ATTEMPTS = 24;\n  const DELAY_MS = 5000;\n  let lastOutput = '';\n  for (let attempt = 1; attempt <= ATTEMPTS; attempt += 1) {\n    nudge(...nudgePaths);\n    await sleep(DELAY_MS);\n    const res = verify(opts);\n    if (res.ok) {\n      return true;\n    }\n    lastOutput = res.output;\n  }\n  console.error(`FAIL: ${label} did not verify after ${ATTEMPTS} attempts`);\n  // Surface the last verifier output so the failure is self-diagnosing instead of\n  // forcing a separate log-forensics pass (the child output was previously swallowed).\n  if (lastOutput) {\n    const indented = lastOutput\n      .split('\\n')\n      .map((line) => `    ${line}`)\n      .join('\\n');\n    console.error(`  last verifier output:\\n${indented}`);\n  }\n  return false;\n}"
}
```

Now I have the mkdir path. The `parent_metadata` snapshot is built synchronously in `build_mkdir_journal_entry`, then the spawned thread publishes the root parent metadata directly (lines 634–697) with a blind `record_publish` on success — bypassing the debounced merge-on-write publisher the file path uses. To design the fix correctly I need the debounced publisher's internals. Let me read those.

```
258	    pub fn get_lock(&self, ipns_name: &str) -> Arc<tokio::sync::Mutex<()>> {
259	        let mut locks = self.publish_locks.lock().unwrap();
260	        locks
261	            .entry(ipns_name.to_string())
262	            .or_insert_with(|| Arc::new(tokio::sync::Mutex::new(())))
263	            .clone()
264	    }
265	
266	    pub async fn resolve_sequence(
267	        &self,
268	        api: &cipherbox_api_client::ApiClient,
269	        ipns_name: &str,
270	    ) -> Result<u64, String> {
271	        match cipherbox_api_client::ipns::resolve_ipns(api, ipns_name).await {
272	            Ok(resp) => {
273	                let resolved = resp.sequence_number.parse::<u64>().unwrap_or_else(|e| {
274	                    log::warn!(
275	                        "Failed to parse IPNS sequence '{}' for {}: {}",
276	                        resp.sequence_number,
277	                        ipns_name,
278	                        e
279	                    );
280	                    0
281	                });
282	                let cached = self.get_cached(ipns_name).unwrap_or(0);
283	                let seq = std::cmp::max(resolved, cached);
284	                self.update_cache(ipns_name, seq);
285	                Ok(seq)
286	            }
287	            Err(e) => match self.get_cached(ipns_name) {
288	                Some(cached) => {
289	                    log::warn!(
290	                        "IPNS resolve failed for {}, using cached seq {}: {}",
291	                        ipns_name,
292	                        cached,
293	                        e
294	                    );
295	                    Ok(cached)
296	                }
297	                None => Err(format!(
298	                    "IPNS resolve failed and no cached sequence for {}: {}",
299	                    ipns_name, e
300	                )),
301	            },
302	        }
303	    }
304	
305	    /// Strict resolve for replay classification: returns Err on ANY resolve failure,
306	    /// never falling back to the cache. A genuine success still updates+returns the
307	    /// max(resolved, cached) sequence so a subsequent confirmed publish advances correctly.
308	    pub async fn resolve_sequence_strict(
309	        &self,
310	        api: &cipherbox_api_client::ApiClient,
311	        ipns_name: &str,
312	    ) -> Result<u64, String> {
313	        let resp = cipherbox_api_client::ipns::resolve_ipns(api, ipns_name)
314	            .await
315	            .map_err(|e| format!("IPNS resolve failed for {}: {}", ipns_name, e))?;
316	        let resolved = resp.sequence_number.parse::<u64>().map_err(|e| {
317	            format!(
318	                "Invalid IPNS sequence '{}' for {}: {}",
319	                resp.sequence_number, ipns_name, e
320	            )
321	        })?;
322	        let cached = self.get_cached(ipns_name).unwrap_or(0);
323	        let seq = std::cmp::max(resolved, cached);
324	        self.update_cache(ipns_name, seq);
325	        Ok(seq)
326	    }
327	
328	    pub fn record_publish(&self, ipns_name: &str, published_seq: u64) {
329	        self.update_cache(ipns_name, published_seq);
330	    }
331	
332	    fn get_cached(&self, ipns_name: &str) -> Option<u64> {
333	        self.seq_cache.lock().unwrap().get(ipns_name).copied()
334	    }
335	
336	    fn update_cache(&self, ipns_name: &str, seq: u64) {
337	        let mut cache = self.seq_cache.lock().unwrap();
338	        let entry = cache.entry(ipns_name.to_string()).or_insert(0);
339	        if seq > *entry {
340	            *entry = seq;
341	        }
342	    }
343	}

345	/// Encrypt a FolderMetadata struct and package as JSON bytes ready for IPFS upload.
346	#[cfg(any(feature = "fuse", feature = "winfsp"))]
347	pub fn encrypt_metadata_to_json(
348	    metadata: &cipherbox_core::folder::FolderMetadata,
349	    folder_key: &[u8],
350	) -> Result<Vec<u8>, String> {
351	    let folder_key_arr: [u8; 32] = folder_key
352	        .try_into()
353	        .map_err(|_| "Invalid folder key length".to_string())?;
354	    let sealed = cipherbox_core::folder::encrypt_folder_metadata(metadata, &folder_key_arr)
355	        .map_err(|e| format!("Metadata encryption failed: {}", e))?;
356	    let iv_hex = hex::encode(&sealed[..12]);
357	    use base64::Engine;
358	    let data_base64 = base64::engine::general_purpose::STANDARD.encode(&sealed[12..]);
359	    let json = serde_json::json!({ "iv": iv_hex, "data": data_base64 });
360	    serde_json::to_vec(&json).map_err(|e| format!("JSON serialization failed: {}", e))
361	}

363	/// Merge local children onto remote children to resolve a concurrent-edit conflict.
364	#[cfg(any(feature = "fuse", feature = "winfsp"))]
365	pub fn merge_folder_children(
366	    local: &cipherbox_core::folder::FolderMetadata,
367	    remote: cipherbox_core::folder::FolderMetadata,
368	) -> cipherbox_core::folder::FolderMetadata {
369	    use cipherbox_core::folder::FolderChild;
370	
371	    fn child_ipns_key(child: &FolderChild) -> &str {
372	        match child {
373	            FolderChild::Folder(f) => f.ipns_name.as_str(),
374	            FolderChild::File(f) => f.file_meta_ipns_name.as_str(),
375	        }
376	    }
377	
378	    let local_by_ipns: std::collections::HashMap<String, &FolderChild> = local
379	        .children
380	        .iter()
381	        .map(|c| (child_ipns_key(c).to_string(), c))
382	        .collect();
383	
384	    let mut merged: Vec<FolderChild> = Vec::new();
385	    let mut seen_ipns: std::collections::HashSet<String> = std::collections::HashSet::new();
386	
387	    for remote_child in &remote.children {
388	        let ipns = child_ipns_key(remote_child).to_string();
389	        if let Some(local_child) = local_by_ipns.get(&ipns) {
390	            merged.push((*local_child).clone());
391	        } else {
392	            merged.push(remote_child.clone());
393	        }
394	        seen_ipns.insert(ipns);
395	    }
396	
397	    for local_child in &local.children {
398	        let ipns = child_ipns_key(local_child).to_string();
399	        if !seen_ipns.contains(&ipns) {
400	            merged.push(local_child.clone());
401	        }
402	    }
403	
404	    cipherbox_core::folder::FolderMetadata {
405	        version: "v2".to_string(),
406	        children: merged,
407	    }
408	}
```

```
412	pub fn spawn_metadata_publish(
413	    api: Arc<ApiClient>,
414	    rt: tokio::runtime::Handle,
415	    metadata: cipherbox_core::folder::FolderMetadata,
416	    folder_key: Vec<u8>,
417	    ipns_private_key: Vec<u8>,
418	    ipns_name: String,
419	    old_metadata_cid: Option<String>,
420	    coordinator: Arc<PublishCoordinator>,
421	) {
422	    std::thread::spawn(move || {
423	        let result = rt.block_on(async {
424	            let lock = coordinator.get_lock(&ipns_name);
425	            let _guard = lock.lock().await;
426	
427	            let json_bytes = encrypt_metadata_to_json(&metadata, &folder_key)?;
428	            let seq = coordinator.resolve_sequence(&api, &ipns_name).await?;
429	            let new_cid = cipherbox_api_client::ipfs::upload_content(&api, &json_bytes)
430	                .await
431	                .map_err(|e| format!("{}", e))?;
432	
433	            let ipns_key_arr: [u8; 32] = ipns_private_key
434	                .try_into()
435	                .map_err(|_| "Invalid IPNS private key length".to_string())?;
436	            let new_seq = seq + 1;
437	            let value = format!("/ipfs/{}", new_cid);
438	            let record =
439	                cipherbox_core::create_ipns_record(&ipns_key_arr, &value, new_seq, 86_400_000)
440	                    .map_err(|e| format!("IPNS record creation failed: {}", e))?;
441	            let marshaled = cipherbox_core::marshal_ipns_record(&record)
442	                .map_err(|e| format!("IPNS record marshal failed: {}", e))?;
443	
444	            use base64::Engine;
445	            let record_b64 = base64::engine::general_purpose::STANDARD.encode(&marshaled);
446	
447	            let req = cipherbox_api_client::IpnsPublishRequest {
448	                ipns_name: ipns_name.clone(),
449	                record: record_b64,
450	                metadata_cid: new_cid.clone(),
451	                encrypted_ipns_private_key: None,
452	                key_epoch: None,
453	                expected_sequence_number: Some(seq.to_string()),
454	            };
455	
456	            match cipherbox_api_client::ipns::publish_ipns(&api, &req)
457	                .await
458	                .map_err(|e| format!("{}", e))?
459	            {
460	                cipherbox_api_client::PublishResult::Success => {
461	                    coordinator.record_publish(&ipns_name, new_seq);
462	                    if let Some(old) = old_metadata_cid {
463	                        let _ = cipherbox_api_client::ipfs::unpin_content(&api, &old).await;
464	                    }
465	                    log::info!("Background metadata publish succeeded for {}", ipns_name);
466	                }
467	                cipherbox_api_client::PublishResult::Conflict {
468	                    current_sequence_number,
469	                } => {
470	                    log::warn!(
471	                        "Conflict for {}: expected seq {}, server has {}",
472	                        ipns_name,
473	                        seq,
474	                        current_sequence_number
475	                    );
476	
477	                    let fresh_seq = coordinator.resolve_sequence(&api, &ipns_name).await?;
478	                    let remote_resolve = cipherbox_api_client::ipns::resolve_ipns(&api, &ipns_name)
479	                        .await
480	                        .map_err(|e| format!("{}", e))?;
481	                    let remote_bytes =

490	                    let merged_metadata = merge_folder_children(&metadata, remote_metadata);
```

> AGENT

`spawn_metadata_publish` also does a **blind overwrite on Success** (merge only on 409 conflict) — so the fix isn't just "route mkdir through it." The real invariant: there must be a *single coalesced* root publisher that builds its snapshot from current in-memory state at flush time. Let me read `queue_publish`, `flush_publish_queue`, `build_folder_metadata`, and the `MkdirConflict` handler to confirm coalescing behavior.

```
1131	    pub fn drain_upload_completions(&mut self) {
1132	        while let Ok(event) = self.upload_rx.try_recv() {
1133	            match event {
1134	                FsEvent::UploadComplete(result) => {
1135	                    if let Some(inode) = self.inodes.get_mut(result.ino) {
1136	                        if inode.write_generation == result.write_generation {
1137	                            if let inode::InodeKind::File { ref mut cid, .. } = inode.kind {
1138	                                *cid = result.new_cid.clone();
1139	                            }
1140	                        }
1141	                    }
1142	                    if let Some(inode) = self.inodes.get(result.ino) {
1143	                        if inode.write_generation == result.write_generation {
1144	                            if let Some(plaintext) = self.pending_content.remove(&result.ino) {
1145	                                self.content_cache.set(&result.new_cid, plaintext);
1146	                            }
1147	                        }
1148	                    }
1149	                    for pruned_cid in &result.pruned_cids {
1150	                        let api = self.api.clone();
1151	                        let cid = pruned_cid.clone();
1152	                        self.rt.spawn(async move {
1153	                            let _ = cipherbox_api_client::ipfs::unpin_content(&api, &cid).await;
1154	                        });
1155	                    }
1156	                    if let Some(entry) = self.publish_queue.get_mut(&result.parent_ino) {
1157	                        entry.pending_uploads = entry.pending_uploads.saturating_sub(1);
1158	                    }
1159	                }
1160	                FsEvent::MkdirConflict { parent_ino } => {
1161	                    // Re-arm the debounced publisher so it retries the parent publish
1162	                    // with a fresh sequence number (D-11a).
1163	                    self.mutated_folders
1164	                        .insert(parent_ino, std::time::Instant::now());
1165	                    self.queue_publish(parent_ino, false);
1166	                }
1167	            }
1168	        }
1169	        self.flush_publish_queue();
1170	    }

1172	    pub fn queue_publish(&mut self, folder_ino: u64, has_pending_upload: bool) {
1173	        let entry = self
1174	            .publish_queue
1175	            .entry(folder_ino)
1176	            .or_insert(PublishQueueEntry {
1177	                first_dirty: std::time::Instant::now(),
1178	                pending_uploads: 0,
1179	            });
1180	        if has_pending_upload {
1181	            entry.pending_uploads += 1;
1182	        }
1183	        self.mutated_folders
1184	            .insert(folder_ino, std::time::Instant::now());
1185	    }
1186	
1187	    fn flush_publish_queue(&mut self) {
1188	        let now = std::time::Instant::now();
1189	        let debounce = std::time::Duration::from_millis(1500);
1190	        let safety_valve = std::time::Duration::from_secs(10);
1191	        let ready: Vec<u64> = self
1192	            .publish_queue
1193	            .iter()
1194	            .filter(|(_, e)| {
1195	                let elapsed = now.duration_since(e.first_dirty);
1196	                elapsed >= safety_valve || (e.pending_uploads == 0 && elapsed >= debounce)
1197	            })
1198	            .map(|(&ino, _)| ino)
1199	            .collect();
1200	        for folder_ino in ready {
1201	            self.publish_queue.remove(&folder_ino);
1202	            match self.build_folder_metadata(folder_ino) {
1203	                Ok((m, fk, ipk, in_, oc)) => spawn_metadata_publish(
1204	                    self.api.clone(),
1205	                    self.rt.clone(),
1206	                    m,
1207	                    fk,
1208	                    ipk,
1209	                    in_,
1210	                    oc,
1211	                    self.publish_coordinator.clone(),
1212	                ),
1213	                Err(e) => log::error!(
1214	                    "Failed to build folder metadata for publish (ino {}): {}",
1215	                    folder_ino,
1216	                    e
1217	                ),
1218	            }
1219	        }
1220	    }

1222	    pub fn drain_refresh_completions(&mut self) {
1223	        let cutoff = std::time::Instant::now() - std::time::Duration::from_secs(30);
1224	        self.mutated_folders.retain(|_, ts| *ts > cutoff);

1227	                PendingRefresh::Success {
1228	                    ino,
1229	                    ipns_name,
1230	                    metadata,
1231	                    cid,
1232	                } => (ino, ipns_name, metadata, cid),
1233	                PendingRefresh::Failure { ipns_name } => {
1234	                    self.refreshing_metadata.remove(&ipns_name);

1236	                }
1237	            };

1251	            ) {
1252	                log::warn!("Drain refresh apply failed for ino {}: {}", ino, e);
1253	            }
1254	            // Spawn async resolution for unresolved FilePointers in this folder

1264	                        Ok(arr) => arr,
1265	                        Err(_) => {
1266	                            log::warn!(
1267	                                "FilePointer resolution skipped for folder ino {}: folder_key length is {} (expected 32)",
1268	                                ino,

1270	                            );
1271	                            continue;
1272	                        }
1273	                    };
1274	                    // Cap concurrent resolution tasks to avoid network thrashing in large folders
1275	                    const MAX_CONCURRENT_FP_RESOLVES: usize = 10;
1276	                    let mut spawned = 0;
1277	                    for (ino, fp_ipns) in unresolved {
1278	                        if self.resolving_file_pointers.contains(&ino) {
1279	                            continue; // Already in-flight
1280	                        }
1281	                        if spawned >= MAX_CONCURRENT_FP_RESOLVES {
1282	                            break; // Remaining will be picked up on next refresh cycle
1283	                        }
1284	                        self.resolving_file_pointers.insert(ino);
1285	                        spawned += 1;

1287	                        let tx = self.filepointer_tx.clone();

1291	                                    .await

1301	                            .await;

1303	                            match result {

1333	                        });
```

> AGENT

The debounced publisher **coalesces** (one `build_folder_metadata` from current in-memory children per flush) — so the question is whether the mkdir's *inline* parent snapshot and the file's debounced snapshot are each complete. That hinges on `build_mkdir_journal_entry`'s snapshot timing, `build_folder_metadata`, and how `handle_create` triggers its publish. Let me read those three — they determine the exact drop mechanism.

> AGENT

`build_folder_metadata` builds from **current** in-memory children (all of them) at call time. So whether the mkdir's parent snapshot is complete depends entirely on `build_mkdir_journal_entry`'s snapshot. This one fact is decisive — if that snapshot is also complete, the lost-update hypothesis is wrong. Let me read it.

```
400	    /// Performs:
401	    /// 1. Generate the initial encrypted folder metadata bytes.
402	    /// 2. Optionally ECIES-wrap the child IPNS key for the TEE.
403	    /// 3. Build the parent folder metadata snapshot via `build_folder_metadata`.
404	    /// 4. ECIES-wrap parent + child IPNS private keys (user key).
405	    /// 5. Build and return a [`JournalOp::MkdirPublish`] entry.
406	    ///
407	    /// # Security
408	    ///
409	    /// Each key is wrapped exactly once.  The TEE-wrapped key is stored
410	    /// separately from the user-ECIES-wrapped key in the result struct.
411	    pub fn build_mkdir_journal_entry(
412	        &self,
413	        parent_ino: u64,
414	        child_ino: u64,
415	        name: &str,
416	        folder_key: &[u8],
417	        ipns_name: &str,
418	        ipns_private_key: zeroize::Zeroizing<Vec<u8>>,
419	        encrypted_folder_key_hex: &str,
420	    ) -> Result<MkdirJournalResult, String> {
421	        let metadata = cipherbox_core::folder::FolderMetadata {
422	            version: "v2".to_string(),
423	            children: vec![],
424	        };
425	
426	        let json_bytes = crate::encrypt_metadata_to_json(&metadata, folder_key)?;
427	
428	        // TEE-wrap the child IPNS private key for republishing (if TEE configured).
429	        let encrypted_ipns_for_tee = if let Some(ref tee_key) = self.tee_public_key {
430	            let wrapped = cipherbox_crypto::wrap_key(&ipns_private_key, tee_key)
431	                .map_err(|e| format!("TEE key wrapping failed: {}", e))?;
432	            Some(hex::encode(&wrapped))
433	        } else {
434	            None
435	        };
436	        let tee_key_epoch = self.tee_key_epoch;
437	
438	        let (parent_metadata, parent_folder_key, parent_ipns_key, parent_ipns_name, parent_old_cid) =
439	            self.build_folder_metadata(parent_ino)?;
440	
441	        let mkdir_created_at_ms = current_unix_ms();
442	
443	        // CR-01: journal the user-ECIES-wrapped parent IPNS key for replay signing.
444	        // An empty string (on wrap failure) parks the entry on replay rather than
445	        // persisting a degraded key silently.
446	        let parent_ipns_key_hex_for_journal =
447	            wrap_key_to_hex(&parent_ipns_key, &self.public_key, "parent IPNS key");
448	
449	        // CR-03: journal the user-ECIES-wrapped child IPNS key (NOT TEE-wrapped) —
450	        // replay writes this into FolderEntry.ipns_private_key_encrypted, which must
451	        // be user-ECIES-wrapped.  An empty string parks the entry rather than
452	        // bricking the folder with an unusable key.
453	        let child_ipns_key_hex_user_wrapped =
454	            wrap_key_to_hex(&ipns_private_key, &self.public_key, "child IPNS key");
455	
456	        // D-04 (IN-03): ECIES-encrypt the directory name at rest. A failed encryption
457	        // fails the write rather than persisting a plaintext or empty name.
458	        let name_encrypted_hex = hex::encode(
459	            cipherbox_crypto::ecies::wrap_key(name.as_bytes(), &self.public_key)
460	                .map_err(|e| format!("directory name encryption failed: {}", e))?,
461	        );
462	
463	        let entry = cipherbox_sdk::JournalEntry {
464	            id: generate_entry_id(),
465	            vault_root_ipns: self.root_ipns_name.clone(),
466	            op: cipherbox_sdk::JournalOp::MkdirPublish {
467	                child_ipns_name: ipns_name.to_string(),
468	                child_folder_key_hex: encrypted_folder_key_hex.to_string(),
469	                child_ipns_key_hex: child_ipns_key_hex_user_wrapped,
```

> AGENT

This is decisive and it **refutes the synthesis's root cause**: `build_mkdir_journal_entry` (line 438) builds its parent snapshot via `build_folder_metadata(parent_ino)`, which iterates the parent's **current** in-memory children — and it's called at handle_mkdir line 549, *after* the file was already added to `root.children` by the earlier `handle_create`. So the mkdir's root snapshot **includes the file**, and so does the file's debounced snapshot. Neither publisher drops the file from root. The "mkdir overwrites a root missing the file" mechanism doesn't hold.

So I should **not** implement that fix — it would target a non-bug in durability-critical code. I need the *real* verify error. The diagnostics fix is already on #537; let me dispatch desktop E2E on #537 to capture it, and meanwhile read the per-file publish path (the likelier suspect: the file's *own* per-file IPNS metadata not getting published when a mkdir races the upload-complete drain).

```
744	    file_meta_ipns_name: String,
745	    file_ipns_private_key: Zeroizing<Vec<u8>>,
746	    source_folder_key: Zeroizing<Vec<u8>>,
747	    dest_folder_key: Zeroizing<Vec<u8>>,
748	    coordinator: Arc<PublishCoordinator>,
749	    tee_public_key: Option<Vec<u8>>,
750	    tee_key_epoch: Option<u32>,
751	) {
752	    // Distinct folders always carry distinct keys; this guard is purely defensive
753	    // and skips a needless resolve+publish (and sequence bump) if they ever match.
754	    if source_folder_key == dest_folder_key {
755	        return;
756	    }
757	    std::thread::spawn(move || {
758	        // Fixed-size keys; a bad length is a terminal misconfiguration, not retryable.
759	        // Wrap the copies in `Zeroizing` so the fixed-size key bytes are wiped on drop.
760	        let source_key_arr: Zeroizing<[u8; 32]> =
761	            match source_folder_key.as_slice().try_into() {
762	                Ok(arr) => Zeroizing::new(arr),
763	                Err(_) => {
764	                    log::error!(
765	                        "File metadata re-encrypt on move failed: invalid source folder key length"
766	                    );
767	                    return;
768	                }
769	            };
770	        let dest_key_arr: Zeroizing<[u8; 32]> = match dest_folder_key.as_slice().try_into() {
771	            Ok(arr) => Zeroizing::new(arr),
772	            Err(_) => {
773	                log::error!(
774	                    "File metadata re-encrypt on move failed: invalid destination folder key length"
775	                );
776	                return;
777	            }
778	        };
779	
780	        let result: Result<(), String> = rt.block_on(async {
781	            let mut last_error = String::new();
782	            for attempt in 1..=REENCRYPT_MAX_ATTEMPTS {
783	                // Serialize against concurrent publishes to the same file IPNS record.
784	                // Re-acquired each attempt so backoff sleeps never hold the lock.
785	                let outcome = {
786	                    let lock = coordinator.get_lock(&file_meta_ipns_name);
787	                    let _guard = lock.lock().await;
788	
789	                    match resolve_and_fetch_file_meta(&api, &file_meta_ipns_name).await {
790	                        Err(e) => ReencryptOutcome::Retry(e),
791	                        Ok(enc_bytes) => {
792	                            // Decrypt the CURRENT record under the SOURCE folder key.
793	                            match cipherbox_core::decrypt_file_metadata_from_ipfs_public(
794	                                &enc_bytes,
795	                                &source_key_arr,
796	                            ) {
797	                                Ok(file_meta) => {
798	                                    // Republish the SAME metadata sealed under the
799	                                    // DESTINATION key. The record already exists, so this
800	                                    // is an update (seq + 1), no TEE enroll.
801	                                    match publish_file_metadata(
802	                                        &api,
803	                                        &file_meta,
804	                                        &dest_folder_key,
805	                                        &file_ipns_private_key,
806	                                        &file_meta_ipns_name,
807	                                        coordinator.as_ref(),
808	                                        tee_public_key.as_deref(),
809	                                        tee_key_epoch,
810	                                        false,
811	                                    )
812	                                    .await
813	                                    {
814	                                        Ok(()) => ReencryptOutcome::Done,
815	                                        Err(e) => ReencryptOutcome::Retry(format!(
816	                                            "re-publish file metadata under dest key: {}",
817	                                            e
818	                                        )),
819	                                    }
820	                                }
821	                                Err(source_err) => {
822	                                    // Source-key decrypt failed. A prior attempt may have
823	                                    // already re-keyed the record to the destination —
824	                                    // confirm under the dest key (idempotent). If THAT also
825	                                    // fails the record is genuinely undecryptable: terminal,
826	                                    // retrying can't recover it.
827	                                    if cipherbox_core::decrypt_file_metadata_from_ipfs_public(
828	                                        &enc_bytes,
829	                                        &dest_key_arr,
830	                                    )
831	                                    .is_ok()
832	                                    {
833	                                        ReencryptOutcome::AlreadyDone
834	                                    } else {
835	                                        ReencryptOutcome::Terminal(format!(
836	                                            "decrypt file metadata under source key: {}",
837	                                            source_err
838	                                        ))
839	                                    }
840	                                }
841	                            }
842	                        }
843	                    }
844	                }; // lock released here, before any backoff sleep
845	
846	                match outcome {
847	                    ReencryptOutcome::Done => {
848	                        log::info!(
849	                            "Re-encrypted file metadata for {} after cross-folder move",
850	                            file_meta_ipns_name
851	                        );
852	                        return Ok(());
853	                    }
854	                    ReencryptOutcome::AlreadyDone => {
855	                        log::info!(
856	                            "File metadata for {} already re-encrypted to destination key",
857	                            file_meta_ipns_name
858	                        );
859	                        return Ok(());
860	                    }
861	                    ReencryptOutcome::Terminal(e) => return Err(e),
862	                    ReencryptOutcome::Retry(e) => {
863	                        last_error = e;
864	                        if attempt < REENCRYPT_MAX_ATTEMPTS {
865	                            // Exponential backoff (0.5s, 1s, 2s, 4s) + jitter, matching the
866	                            // publish-conflict backoff idiom elsewhere in this file.
867	                            let base_ms = 500u64 << (attempt - 1);
868	                            let jitter_ms = (std::time::SystemTime::now()
869	                                .duration_since(std::time::UNIX_EPOCH)
870	                                .unwrap_or_default()
871	                                .subsec_nanos()
872	                                % 400) as u64;
873	                            tokio::time::sleep(std::time::Duration::from_millis(
874	                                base_ms + jitter_ms,
875	                            ))
876	                            .await;
877	                        }
878	                    }
879	                }
880	            }
881	            Err(format!(
882	                "exhausted {} attempts: {}",
883	                REENCRYPT_MAX_ATTEMPTS, last_error
884	            ))
885	        });
886	        if let Err(e) = result {
887	            log::error!("File metadata re-encrypt on move failed: {}", e);
888	        }
889	    });
890	}
```

> AGENT

I've now read the full publish chain, and the code refutes the synthesized root cause:

- The mkdir's parent snapshot is built via `build_folder_metadata` (complete, includes the file).
- The new file's *parent* publish is the debounced publisher (also a complete snapshot); the new file's *per-file* metadata publishes on its own IPNS (the mkdir never touches it).
- Even with sequence churn, the final root metadata is complete, and both the root and per-file publish chains self-heal on conflict.

So **"mkdir overwrites a root missing the file" does not hold** — and from static reading I can't find a mechanism where the racing mkdir actually breaks the file's resolvability. I'm not going to make a speculative change to data-loss-critical durability code on a refuted hypothesis. I need the verifier's actual error.

That's exactly what the diagnostics commit surfaces. I dispatched desktop E2E on #537 (run 27889806785); when it fails it'll now print the verifier's real stdout/stderr (e.g. "file not found in root" vs "per-file IPNS resolve failed" vs "content decrypt mismatch"). Let me watch it and auto-extract that output.

Status — and an important course-correction:

**I refuted the workflow's root cause via code review, so I did not implement that fix.** Reading the actual FUSE source on this branch:

- The mkdir's parent snapshot is built by `build_folder_metadata(parent)` *after* the file was added to `root.children` → it **includes the file**. The new file's own parent publish is the debounced publisher (also a complete snapshot).
- So neither root publisher drops the file; even with sequence churn the final root metadata is complete, and both the root and per-file publish paths self-heal on 409 conflict.

In other words, "mkdir publishes a root missing the file" doesn't hold up — and from static reading I can't find a mechanism where the racing mkdir actually breaks the file's resolvability. I'm not going to patch data-loss-critical durability code on a hypothesis the code contradicts.

**To get the real cause, I'm using the diagnostics I already added to #537.** I dispatched desktop E2E on this branch (run [27889806785](https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/actions/runs/27889806785)); when move-content fails it will now print the verifier's actual error — which tells us definitively whether it's "file not found in root", "per-file IPNS resolve failed", or "content/decrypt mismatch". I'm watching it in the background (~10 min) and will implement the precise fix on this branch once I see it.

So: the FUSE work is in progress, but pointed at the *real* error instead of a misdiagnosis. I'll report the verifier output the moment the run lands.

> AGENT

**The diagnostics nailed it instantly — and it's neither suspect's fault, nor a FUSE bug.** The verifier's real error:

```
/home/runner/work/cipher-box/cipher-box/node_modules/.bin/tsx:2
basedir=$(dirname "$(echo "$0" | sed -e 's,\\,/,g')")
          ^^^^^^^
SyntaxError: missing ) after argument list
    at wrapSafe (node:internal/modules/cjs/loader...)
```

`test-move-content.ts`'s `verify()` spawns `node node_modules/.bin/tsx <script>` — but `.bin/tsx` is a **shell shim**, not JavaScript. Node tries to parse the shell script as JS → `SyntaxError` on **every** one of the 24 attempts → "file not decryptable." The other tests pass because they use `pnpm exec tsx` (the shim run as a shell script), not `node <shim>`.

So move-content is a pure **test-infra bug from the #532 migration** — no FUSE race, no product change, no release bump. Fixable right here on #537. Let me reproduce locally and confirm the fix.

```
100	  ];
101	  if (folderName) {
102	    cliArgs.push('--folder-name', folderName);
103	  }
104	  // process.execPath (node) cannot run a .ts file directly, so spawn the tsx
105	  // shim from node_modules/.bin as the interpreter for verify-filepointer.mts.
106	  const result = spawnSync('node', [join(REPO_ROOT, 'node_modules/.bin/tsx'), ...cliArgs], {
107	    env: { ...process.env, TEST_SECRET: secret },
108	    encoding: 'utf8',
109	    // Bound the child: a stalled verify must not hang the whole suite — pollVerify
110	    // cannot recover from a blocking spawnSync. A timeout returns a non-zero status
111	    // so the poll loop treats it as a failed attempt and retries.
112	    timeout: 60_000,
113	    maxBuffer: 1024 * 1024,
114	  });
115	  if (result.error) {
116	    return { ok: false, output: `verify-filepointer failed to execute: ${result.error.message}` };
117	  }
118	  // The verifier never prints secrets (it refuses --secret; TEST_SECRET is env-only),
119	  // so its stdout/stderr are safe to surface for diagnostics on final failure.
120	  const output = [result.stdout, result.stderr].filter(Boolean).join('\n').trim();
121	  return { ok: result.status === 0, output };
```

> AGENT

Here's where it landed — and it's a clean outcome.

## Real root cause (found via the diagnostics)

move-content's `verify()` spawned `node node_modules/.bin/tsx <script>`, but `.bin/tsx` is a **POSIX shell shim** — node parsed it as JavaScript and threw `SyntaxError` on **all 24** pre-move verify attempts → "file not decryptable." The other suites use `pnpm exec tsx` (shim run as a shell script), so they were never affected.

**It's a test-infra bug from the #532 TS migration — not the FUSE hardening, not a product regression, not a publish race.** Both suspects (and the workflow's lost-update theory) were wrong; I'd already refuted the FUSE theory from the source, and the diagnostics confirmed the real cause. So **no FUSE/product change and no separate `fix(desktop)` PR are needed.**

## Fix (on #537, `ed7515d81`)

Invoke tsx's real JS CLI (`node_modules/tsx/dist/cli.mjs`) via `process.execPath` instead of the shell shim — cross-platform, no PATH/pnpm dependency. Verified locally: the verifier now actually executes (reaches the API) instead of crashing.

## #537 now contains (all `chore(e2e)`, zero release bumps)
1. `.mts` rename — fixes the ESM/CJS `ipns` module-resolution crash
2. drop stale `*.ts` tsconfig glob (CodeRabbit)
3. surface swallowed verifier output on failure (the diagnostics that cracked this)
4. run move-content verifier via tsx CLI, not the bin shim

## Now
Confirmation desktop E2E dispatched on HEAD: [27890112936](https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/actions/runs/27890112936). Watching in the background. If desktop goes green, #537 meets your merge condition and is ready — I'll report the result.
